=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recepies: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        dct=defaultdict(lambda :[])
        indegree={}
        n=len(recepies)

        for i in recepies:
            indegree[i]=0

        for i in range(n):
            for j in ingredients[i]:
                indegree[j]=0

        for i in range(n):
            for j in ingredients[i]:
                dct[j].append(recepies[i])
                indegree[recepies[i]]+=1

        st=[]
        for i in indegree:
            if indegree[i]==0:
                st.append(i)
        flst=[]
        ans=defaultdict(lambda :[])
        while st:
            x=st.pop(0)
            for i in dct[x]:
                # if ans[x]!=[]:
                for j in ans[x]:
                    if j not in ans[i]:
                        ans[i].append(j)
                ans[i].append(x)
                indegree[i]-=1
                if indegree[i]==0:
                    st.append(i)
            if x in recepies:
                for k in ans[x]:
                    if k not in supplies:
                        break
                else:
                    flst.append(x)
                    supplies.append(i)

        return flst

```python
class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recepies: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        dct=defaultdict(lambda :[])
        indegree={}
        n=len(recepies)

        for i in recepies:
            indegree[i]=0

        for i in range(n):
            for j in ingredients[i]:
                indegree[j]=0

        for i in range(n):
            for j in ingredients[i]:
                dct[j].append(recepies[i])
                indegree[recepies[i]]+=1

        st=[]
        for i in indegree:
            if indegree[i]==0:
                st.append(i)
        flst=[]
        ans=defaultdict(lambda :[])
        while st:
            x=st.pop(0)
            for i in dct[x]:
                # if ans[x]!=[]:
                for j in ans[x]:
                    if j not in ans[i]:
                        ans[i].append(j)
                ans[i].append(x)
                indegree[i]-=1
                if indegree[i]==0:
                    st.append(i)
            if x in recepies:
                for k in ans[x]:
                    if k not in supplies:
                        break
                else:
                    flst.append(x)
                    supplies.append(i)

        return flst
```

Let's understand original code.

```python
class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recepies: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        dct=defaultdict(lambda :[])
        indegree={}
        n=len(recepies)

        for i in recepies:
            indegree[i]=0

        for i in range(n):
            for j in ingredients[i]:
                indegree[j]=0

        for i in range(n):
            for j in ingredients[i]:
                dct[j].append(recepies[i])
                indegree[recepies[i]]+=1

        st=[]
        for i in indegree:
            if indegree[i]==0:
                st.append(i)
        flst=[]
        ans=defaultdict(lambda :[])
        while st:
            x=st.pop(0)
            for i in dct[x]:
                # if ans[x]!=[]:
                for j in ans[x]:
                    if j not in ans[i]:
                        ans[i].append(j)
                ans[i].append(x)
                indegree[i]-=1
                if indegree[i]==0:
                    st.append(i)
            if x in recepies:
                for k in ans[x]:
                    if k not in supplies:
                        break
                else:
                    flst.append(x)
                    supplies.append(i)
        return flst
```

```
graph = defaultdict(list)
indegree = defaultdict(int)
for recipe, ing in zip(recipes, ingredients):
    for i in ing:
        graph[i].append(recipe)
    indegree[recipe] = len(ing)
q = deque(supplies)
ans = []
while q:
    x = q.popleft()
    if x in recipe_set:
        ans.append(x)
    for r in graph[x]:
        indegree[r] -= 1
        if indegree[r] == 0:
            q.append(r)
return ans
```

```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recipes: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        recipe_set = set(recipes)
        indegree = {recipe: len(ingredients[i]) for i, recipe in enumerate(recipes)}
        graph = defaultdict(list)

        for recipe, ing_list in zip(recipes, ingredients):
            for ing in ing_list:
                graph[ing].append(recipe)

        available = set(supplies)
        q = deque(supplies)

        for recipe in recipes:
            if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                q.append(recipe)
                available.add(recipe)

        ans = []
        while q:
            item = q.popleft()
            if item in recipe_set:
                ans.append(item)
            for recipe in graph[item]:
                indegree[recipe] -= 1
                if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                    q.append(recipe)
                    available.add(recipe)

        return ans
```

Code:

```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recipes: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        recipe_set = set(recipes)
        indegree = {recipe: len(ingredients[i]) for i, recipe in enumerate(recipes)}
        graph = defaultdict(list)

        for recipe, ing_list in zip(recipes, ingredients):
            for ing in ing_list:
                graph[ing].append(recipe)

        available = set(supplies)
        q = deque(available)

        for recipe in recipes:
            if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                q.append(recipe)
                available.add(recipe)

        ans = []
        while q:
            item = q.popleft()
            if item in recipe_set:
                ans.append(item)
            for recipe in graph[item]:
                indegree[recipe] -= 1
                if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                    q.append(recipe)
                    available.add(recipe)

        return ans
```

C indegree 2 (B,A) -> after A decrement 1. Pop B ans B, C indegree 0 add C. Pop C ans. Good.

B not added. C not added. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `available` set to prevent duplicate q additions for recipes that are in supplies. Suppose a recipe R is in supplies, q includes R. Later, R's indegree becomes 0, we skip adding. Good. But what if R is in supplies and also has indegree 0, and q initialized with available set. If R is in available, q includes R. Good.

What if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree 0, we add to q and available. Later, if R is also an ingredient for another recipe, when R popped, graph[R] decrements. Good. If R is in available, and later some other ingredient? No.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, but all its ingredients are supplies. It will be added when last ingredient processed. At that time, `recipe not in available` true, so q append and available add. Good.

Potential issue: If a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is in supplies. S is in q. When S popped, R indegree decremented. Good. If S is in supplies but also has indegree >0, it is still in q. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that cannot be made. S not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies. Not in q, so R indegree never reaches 0. Good.

This is correct.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? Contradiction. If R in supplies, it's available. Good.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's think about if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a non-recipe not in supplies, but R is in supplies? no.

Now, let's consider if a recipe R is not in supplies, indegree >0, and one of its ingredients is a recipe S that is not in supplies but can be made. S will be added to q when its indegree reaches 0. When S popped, R decremented. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
final with code only.</think>```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recipes: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        recipe_set = set(recipes)
        indegree = {recipe: len(ingredients[i]) for i, recipe in enumerate(recipes)}
        graph = defaultdict(list)

        for recipe, ing_list in zip(recipes, ingredients):
            for ing in ing_list:
                graph[ing].append(recipe)

        available = set()
        q = deque()

        for s in supplies:
            if s not in available:
                available.add(s)
                q.append(s)

        for recipe in recipes:
            if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                q.append(recipe)
                available.add(recipe)

        ans = []
        while q:
            item = q.popleft()

            if item in recipe_set:
                ans.append(item)

            for recipe in graph[item]:
                indegree[recipe] -= 1
                if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                    q.append(recipe)
                    available.add(recipe)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def findAllRecipes(self, recipes: List[str], ingredients: List[List[str]], supplies: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        recipe_set = set(recipes)
        indegree = {recipe: len(ingredients[i]) for i, recipe in enumerate(recipes)}
        graph = defaultdict(list)

        for recipe, ing_list in zip(recipes, ingredients):
            for ing in ing_list:
                graph[ing].append(recipe)

        available = set()
        q = deque()

        for s in supplies:
            if s not in available:
                available.add(s)
                q.append(s)

        for recipe in recipes:
            if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                q.append(recipe)
                available.add(recipe)

        ans = []
        while q:
            item = q.popleft()

            if item in recipe_set:
                ans.append(item)

            for recipe in graph[item]:
                indegree[recipe] -= 1
                if indegree[recipe] == 0 and recipe not in available:
                    q.append(recipe)
                    available.add(recipe)

        return ans
